## Skills

## Skill: .agents/skills/mcpe-context7/SKILL.md

```markdown
---
name: mcpe-context7
description: Use before looking up ANY Bedrock API member, vanilla id, texture, recipe, component, manifest field or version for the MCPE-Curseforges repo. Routes each need to one of the three Context7 libraries and says when a script must come from the kit zip instead.
---

# mcpe-context7: Where to Look Things Up

## Authority

- `INSTRUCTIONS.md` 1.16 (copy in the kit zip's instruction files) is the policy. This skill is only its routing table and never overrides it.
- Repo rules, scripts and pack files come from the **kit zip** (`New-Pack.zip`, `Maintenance.zip` or the pack's review zip), NOT from Context7. The zip is always fresher.

## Libraries (ONLY these three, IDs exactly, `query-docs` only)

- `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator`: `@minecraft/server` and `@minecraft/server-ui` API, JSON schemas, stable and module versions.
- `/mojang/bedrock-samples`: vanilla ids, textures, recipes, components, entities, formats, `version.json`.
- `/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev`: manifest structure, scripting guides, techniques.

NEVER call `resolve-library-id`. NEVER query any other library.

## Repo lookups (from the kit zip, NOT Context7)

Repo rules, scripts, instruction files, `DOCS_RULES.md`, `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md`, `docs/mcicons-index.md`, `docs/bedrock-guide.md` and all script files come from the kit zip under `files/`. Read them from there. Context7 no longer indexes this repo.

## Rules

1. Query first, write second. Batch: at most ONE `query-docs` call per needed `libraryId`, covering every topic needed from it. NEVER two calls to the same `libraryId`. Name the exact member, file, id or schema and the game or module version. All three calls may go in one parallel turn.
2. Verify every import, class, method, property and JSON field against the retrieved text. NEVER `mojang-minecraft`, `world.events` or 1.x syntax.
3. Empty or doubtful result: no sequential retry loop. Say "not verified". NEVER fill the gap from memory.
4. Versions come only from retrieved text. State the exact version used and the query date.
5. Handoff bullet: `Docs queried (YYYY-MM-DD): <library IDs and topics>`.
6. Library IDs, query text and the word Context7 NEVER go into shipped files or player-facing docs.

## Scripts that must run

- `make_pack_script.py`, `build_payload.py`, `import_project_payload.py`, `doc_template.py`, `pixel_json_to_png.py`, `make_update_script.py` and `apply_update.py` need their COMPLETE unchanged text.
- The complete files are in the kit zip (`New-Pack.zip`, `Maintenance.zip` or the pack's review zip `<Pack>.zip`) under `files/`. Unzip, run from there, NEVER retype or edit them.
- No zip: stop and ask the user to attach the zip. NEVER rewrite a script from memory.

## Icons (MCicons)

- Any vanilla item or block icon (recipe diagrams, sprites, README or listing images, UI art): use MCicons ONLY. It is the release file `reference/mcicons/mcicons-<ver>.tgz` in the repo (`package/dist/icons/minecraft_<id>.png`, 64x64, only `minecraft:` ids). Read it with `scripts/lib/mcicons.py` (`has_icon(id)`, `icon_image(id)`), never retype or guess a PNG.
- No icon for the id (custom ids, newer items): report it. NO fallback to Samples, the Wiki, another icon site or a third-party pack.
- `docs/mcicons-index.md` (in the kit zip under `files/`) lists every icon id it holds: check it before using an icon.
- The `.tgz` itself is not in the kit zips. To run code that reads the pixels it must be in the clone; if it is missing, ask the user to attach it. NEVER substitute a lookalike.
- Every manifest (BP and RP) carries `metadata.authors` = `["ALNStudios"]` and `metadata.product_type` = `"addon"`, always.
- Keep its `ASSET-NOTICE.md` with any redistributed icon.

## Reading the directory map

- The map lists directories with file counts. Pick the folder, then read the file from the zip. For exact file text of a pack, ask for a Collect Files export of that path.
- NEVER edit generated folders and files: `builds/`, `Package/`, `changelog_views/`, `MCPEDL/AI Review/`, `MCPEDL/README.md`, `builds/README.md`.
```

## Skill: .agents/skills/mcpe-pack-update/SKILL.md

````markdown
---
name: mcpe-pack-update
description: Use for ANY change to an existing project, workflow, script or doc in the MCPE-Curseforges repository (update, fix, add, rename). NEVER use the new-pack base64 payload for existing projects. Delivers every change as ONE self-contained Termux update script (<Pack>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh) built with make_update_script.py.
---

# mcpe-pack-update: Update Delivery

## !! CRITICAL: NEVER base64 for existing packs !!

This skill is for **existing projects only**. When the user asks for a change to a pack that already exists in `MCPEDL/`, the ONLY valid delivery is a self-contained `.sh` update script built with `scripts/make_update_script.py`. NEVER deliver:
- A base64 payload or `.b64` file
- A new-pack `.sh` from `make_pack_script.py`
- A raw `.zip`, `.mcaddon`, `.mcpack`, `.mcworld`, `.json`, or `.js`
- Inline pasted file content instead of a `.sh`

If you are unsure whether the target project already exists, check `MCPEDL/` first. If it exists, use this skill. If it truly does not exist, stop and use `mcpe-new-pack` instead.

## Authority

- `INSTRUCTIONS.md` is the policy source. `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is its standalone copy for generation. The script flow is `INSTRUCTIONS.md` "Termux Script Rules", built with `scripts/make_update_script.py`. Where to look each rule up: the `mcpe-context7` skill. This skill is a short working checklist and never restates or overrides them.
- **Precedence** (as in `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md`): factual accuracy and the user's requested scope, then Part 1, then Part 2, then this skill. Part 4 of the generator file never overrides Parts 1 and 2.
- If these files disagree with each other or with a script in `scripts/`, tell the user exactly what differs instead of choosing silently. If `INSTRUCTIONS.md` cannot be read, stop and report it instead of working from memory.
- Achievements line (every project type): always `Yes, achievement-friendly.` Keep any cheats or world-setting caveat as a following sentence.

## Scope

- Use for changes to an existing project (`MCPEDL/Addons|Maps|Script|Minigame/<Name>/`), a workflow, a script, a catalog or any repo doc.
- Read-only audit or report: use `checkrm`.
- New project from an idea: follow `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` Part 4 (ask the type first, base64 payload).

## Reading Source Files Without the AI Review Zip

When working from a direct conversation (not an AI Review export), read the pack's source files from the repo clone directly:
- Manifests: `MCPEDL/<Category>/<Name>/behavior_pack/manifest.json`, `resource_pack/manifest.json`
- Scripts: `MCPEDL/<Category>/<Name>/behavior_pack/scripts/main.js` (and `lib/`)
- Docs: `MCPEDL/<Category>/<Name>/CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md`, `DESCRIPTION.md`
- Policy scripts: `scripts/make_update_script.py`, `scripts/apply_update.py` (read from repo, run them locally)

NEVER skip reading the real source. NEVER work from memory or a previous session's cached content.

## Procedure

1. Read `INSTRUCTIONS.md` Parts 1 and 2 before writing anything, through Context7 as the `mcpe-context7` skill says. `make_update_script.py`, `apply_update.py`, and `pixel_json_to_png.py` are complete in the pack's review zip (`<Pack>.zip`) under `files/`; if the zip is not available, read the scripts directly from `scripts/` in the repo clone.
2. Find the destination folder: the path the user named, otherwise the matching project folder. Do not substitute a similar name; report ambiguity or a missing target.
3. Read the real source (manifests, scripts, items, recipes, loot tables, functions, lang files) before writing any claim. Source priority when sources disagree: the project's own files, Mojang Bedrock Samples, official Minecraft Creator Documentation, Minecraft Wiki, Bedrock Wiki.
4. Make the smallest possible change. No unrelated rewrites. Never invent features, versions, links, assets, test results or compatibility. Never write comments in shipped `.js`, `.mcfunction` or `.json` files.
5. Generate the spec, convert it with `make_update_script.py` into a single self-contained `<Pack>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh` file, and provide the one-line run command for the user.

## The update script

Generate the spec with `apply_update.py` directives (replace, replace-all, insert-after, insert-before, create, overwrite, delete, changelog).
Then build the single `.sh` using:
```bash
python3 scripts/make_update_script.py spec.txt --out-dir .
```
(If running from an extracted kit zip: `python3 files/scripts/make_update_script.py spec.txt --out-dir .`)

This produces ONE single `.sh` file (`<PackName>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh`) that contains the embedded update payload, ANSI colored status UI, automated git rebase, validation checks, commit, and push.

## Reply

1. Attach or output the single `.sh` file (`<PackName>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh`).
2. Give the single Termux command:
```bash
bash ~/storage/downloads/<PackName>_v<Version>_*.sh
```
3. Brief summary of what changed and what checks ran.
4. **Save in AI memory**: Every finished output, applied change summary, and spec version must be retained in AI memory/scratch notes so context is preserved across turns without relying on session replay.
````

# AI Instructions & Review Notes

Repo: https://github.com/AllensCreations/MCPE-Curseforges.git (branch main)
Use Context7 to query this repository's documentation (`docs/`) for project scripts, coding instructions, and Bedrock conventions before reviewing the pack changes. Batch your lookup into a single Context7 query.
Delivery steps: the `mcpe-pack-update` skill (new project: `mcpe-new-pack`).

## Action Notes: Package Update Notes
- **Mode**: UPDATE existing package (CURRENT VERSION: v9 / NEXT UPDATE: v10).
- NEVER use a base64 payload or new-pack `.sh` for an existing pack. This is an UPDATE.
- Retain all UUIDs in manifests. NEVER regenerate UUIDs.
- Update CHANGELOG.md with a new release entry at top under `---`.
- Deliver ALL changes as ONE self-contained update `.sh` (`<Pack>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh`) built with `make_update_script.py` (INSTRUCTIONS.md Reply Format).
- Read the pack's real source files (manifests, scripts, lang, recipes) from this export before writing any change.
- Need command formats or templates? Query Context7 for `make_update_script.py` and `DOCS_RULES.md`.

## Destination
Repo-root-relative folders for this work:
- MCPEDL/Maps/One-Block-Skyblock-Lucky-Blocks
- .sh paths start at repo root, stay inside these folders. New files go here too.
- First folder -> `DEST=` variable at script top. Use it for every path and `git add`.

## Rules
1. NEVER invent features, versions, links, assets, test results.
2. Output only what changed. Smallest edits. No unrelated rewrites.
3. No notes or summaries unless asked.
4. NEVER edit generated files: `builds/`, `Package/`, `changelog_views/`, `MCPEDL/AI Review/`, `MCPEDL/README.md`.
5. NEVER use a base64 payload or new-pack `.sh` for an existing pack update. Use `make_update_script.py` only.

## Reply Format (STRICT, same as INSTRUCTIONS.md "Reply Format")
```
[SYSTEM_CONFIG]
Mode: Direct Execution
Token_Efficiency: Maximum
Rules: no greeting, intro, outro, offers, restating. High technical accuracy.
```
Output ONLY these three sections in order:
1. **File Needed:** repo-root-relative path of each file created/edited.
2. **Changelog:** bullets, brief summary of changes.
3. **Termux Script:** ONE fenced `bash` block holding the ready-to-run commands, and ONE generated self-contained `<Pack>_v<Version>_<rand>.sh` update script (built with `make_update_script.py`). The script contains the full UI, automatically pulls, applies edits safely, commits, and pushes.

## Policy Docs

## Policy Doc: INSTRUCTIONS.md

````markdown
# Instructions: Core Repository Policy (v47)



Repo policy, docs rules for AllensCreations/MCPE-Curseforges (creator name `ALNStudios`). Generating a new project: `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md`.

## Read Only What the Task Needs
| Task | Read | Sections |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Docs (README/DESCRIPTION/CHANGELOG) | this file + `DOCS_RULES.md` | 1.1 to 1.3; 2.1 to 2.5 |
| Docs audit (`/checkrm`) | this file + `DOCS_RULES.md` | 1.1; all of `DOCS_RULES.md` |
| Version, manifest, LOCK, recipe, function, build, workflow, OneBlock changes | this file | 1.1 to 1.12 |
| New mechanic hits a vanilla limit | this file | 1.12 |
| Script API code, manifests, textures, error fixes | this file | 1.2, 1.15, 1.16, 1.17 |
| Any API, vanilla id, version or repo-script lookup | this file | 1.17 (Context7) |
| Recipe images, sprites | this file + `DOCS_RULES.md` | 1.5; 3.3, 3.4 |
| New addon / map / script / minigame | `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` | all |
Read only the files and sections the task needs. Changing an instruction file: read both.

## Reply Format (STRICT: repo edits, updates, `.sh` deliveries)
`[SYSTEM_CONFIG] Direct Execution. Maximum token efficiency. No greeting, intro, outro, offers, restating. High technical accuracy.`
Questions, reviews, discussion: free-form.
Output ONLY, in order, exact headings:
1. **File Needed:** repo-root-relative path of each file created/edited. Update to an existing project or the repo: the generated self-contained update `.sh` (`<Repofilename>_<Version>_<Rand>.sh`).
2. **Changelog:** bullets, brief.
3. **Termux Script:** ONE fenced `bash` block holding ONLY the run commands. Update: `bash ~/storage/downloads/<Repofilename>_<Version>_*.sh` (the self-contained `.sh` with embedded update payload and terminal UI is attached as a downloadable file, NEVER pasted). New pack: `bash ~/storage/downloads/<file>.sh`, the pack `.sh` attached. Commands live in this block only. NEVER a Summary section.
- NEVER text before, between, after. NEVER rename/merge/reorder/skip a section. Nothing to report: `- None`.
- Ask only when 1.1 allows, one line; else proceed with defaults.
- `/checkrm` audit report goes under **Changelog**: projects audited or updated, checks run and results, gaps.
- New-pack build: same three sections, File Needed = the pack `.sh` (`PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` 4.11). Planning phase: present the ~90% research plan and await user approval before proceeding to build.

## Termux Script Rules
Update to an existing project or the repo: the agent builds and delivers ONE self-contained update `.sh` (`<Repofilename>_<Version>_<Rand>.sh`) generated via `scripts/make_update_script.py` (which embeds the update payload inside the script with ANSI status UI). New pack: the pack `.sh` named per 1.1, attached as a downloadable file. Order:
1. `#!/data/data/com.termux/files/usr/bin/bash` + `set -e`
2. `cd "$HOME/MCPE-Curseforges"` (clone first if missing)
3. Discard the generated folders (`git checkout -- Package changelog_views`, CI rebuilds them), then `git pull --rebase --autostash`.
4. Apply ALL changes inside the script. PROJECT UPDATE = search and replace: write a spec and run `python3 scripts/make_update_script.py spec.txt` (format in `scripts/apply_update.py`: `@@ replace`/`replace-all`/`insert-after`/`insert-before`/`delete`/`append`/`create`, exact match, atomic, idempotent; `--verify <clone>` dry-runs it). Size decides the method. SMALL update (a few edits, under about 60% of any file touched): search and replace only. LARGE update (most of a file, many new files, a redesign): use the best method, `@@ create`/`@@ overwrite` in the same spec, never many fragile edits. Deliver ONE self-contained `<Repofilename>_<Version>_<Rand>.sh` built with `scripts/make_update_script.py` (embedding the spec inside it, with ANSI colors, status banner, rebase, validations, commit, push, and self-cleanup). A new pixel texture: `@@ create` its pixel JSON under `texture_sources/`, then `python3 scripts/pixel_json_to_png.py <project>` after the `.sh`. The tool also sets the manifest version, validates JSON/JS, writes both changelogs. Deliver ONLY the `.sh`: NEVER a separate `.zip`, pack file (`.mcaddon`/`.mcworld`/`.mcpack`), `.json`, `.js` or `.b64`. A NEW project is one pack `.sh` from `scripts/make_pack_script.py` (`PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` Part 4).
5. Validate every changed `.json` with python3. Stop on error.
6. Run `python3 scripts/check_instruction_files.py` when an instruction file changed.
6b. Project update: after the CHANGELOG entry, run `python3 scripts/manifest_tools.py --project "$DEST" --set vX` (or `X.0.0`), then `--check`
 and `python3 scripts/validate_manifest_titles.py --project "$DEST"`. Stop on error.
7. `git add` changed paths only; `git commit -m "<short>"`; `git push`
8. Idempotent: skip applied changes.
9. Add an entry to the root `CHANGELOG.md` (repo-wide, newest first under `---`): `## [vN] - YYYY-MM-DD - [Update Name]` (N = `REPO_VERSION` + 1, a single whole number such as v80, no dots; set by `apply_update.py`, never by hand; a repo-level script's `@@ version` (just the number) and its `.sh` name `Repo_vN_<Rand>.sh` MUST equal it, the script refuses otherwise; pack versions use vX
), then `### Added` / `### Improved & Balanced` / `### Fixed` with `- **Title**: what changed`. Every script does this, tooling-only changes included.
10. After a successful push, delete the script itself: `rm -f -- "$SELF"` (SELF = absolute path saved before `cd`; last line, only reached when everything above passed).
Every project update writes CHANGELOG.md (Part 2 format; heading version == `.sh` name version). Manifest versions: 1.3. NEVER edit README download links by hand (build aligns them).
- Spec ledger: the root `CHANGELOG.md` logs delivered repo-level specs: newest first, `## [vN]` = the spec number, with a `**Spec**: Repo_vN_<rand>.sh | **Target** | **Files edited**` line added by `apply_update.py`. Pack release notes live ONLY in the pack's own `CHANGELOG.md` with pack versions. Any AI agent starts an update from the pack's own files and version. A spec that is not in `CHANGELOG.md` is NOT applied: never assume it was.

## Scripts: Agent vs Termux
- `scripts/` is reference: read it to learn the workflow. The Termux `.sh` and CI run it.
- Agents MAY run only: test scripts (`scripts/test_*.py`), `scripts/selftest_project_types.py`, `scripts/make_update_script.py` (build the `.sh`, `--verify`), `scripts/check_instruction_files.py`.
- Agents NEVER run scripts that change the repo (build, bump, publish, delete, import, sync, rename).
- Project checks (`check_readme_status.py`, `validate_manifest_titles.py`, `manifest_tools.py --check`): the agent reads and reports; the `.sh` runs them.

## Precedence
Part 1 wins. `DOCS_RULES.md` never overrides it. Generator file never overrides this file. Accuracy and requested scope outrank any template. Section numbers refer to this file.

---

## Part 1: Core Policy

### 1.1 Accuracy
- ALWAYS inspect source files before claims. Ask only if an unresolved choice affects gameplay, compatibility, distribution, data loss, or versioning. Else use defaults.
- NEVER invent features, versions, links, assets, test results, compatibility, achievement support, performance, platform behavior. Separate verified facts from assumptions.
- NEVER claim in-game test/function run/gameplay result unless tested in Minecraft. Archive/JSON/syntax checks prove structure only.
- Source priority (report disagreements, NEVER choose silently). Read sources 2, 3 and 5 through Context7 (1.17): 1 add-on's own files; 1b MCicons release file `reference/mcicons/mcicons-<ver>.tgz` (NOT in Context7; ids in `docs/mcicons-index.md`; the ONLY source of vanilla item and block icons, no fallback); 2 Mojang Bedrock Samples (`/mojang/bedrock-samples`; offline copy reference/bedrock-samples/bedrock-samples.min.json.gz): vanilla ids, textures, recipes, components, formats;
 3 Minecraft Creator Docs (`/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator`): stable versions, API; 4 Minecraft Wiki (https://minecraft.wiki/, not in Context7, only through a web tool when one exists): display names, edition, icon look; 5 Bedrock Wiki (`/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev`): formats, techniques. Sources 4-5 NEVER override 1-3 on ids, versions, API.
- NEVER comments in shipped `.js`, `.mcfunction`, `.json`. Explanations go in docs or handoff.
- NEVER imply Mojang/Microsoft endorsement.
- `.sh` names: `(Repofilename)_(Version)_(Rand).sh`. Repofilename = project/folder/file changed, no extension, no spaces (hyphens). Version = new `vX` (e.g. `v2`, or legacy `X.Y.Z`), instruction version (`v5`) or repo version (`v80`). Rand = >=5 random lowercase/digits, fresh each time (`head -c 200 /dev/urandom | tr -dc 'a-z0-9' | head -c 6`), NEVER reused. Example `Dirtbound_v2_k7q2x.sh`. Same name in reply and run command.


### 1.2 Versions and Compatibility
Latest stable:
- ALWAYS look up latest stable Bedrock release and Script API module versions at time of work. Record date. NEVER trust numbers in this file or memory.
- Refs: Context7 `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator` (Script API versioning, `@minecraft/server` and `@minecraft/server-ui` stable references, platform version guidance), queried per 1.17. Community wikis NEVER pick versions or confirm API.
- Pick highest stable module version supported by target game and every dependency (check peers). NEVER preview/beta/RC unless a requested feature needs it; then disclose in docs and handoff.
- Game version and module version are separate. NEVER infer one from the other.

Engine:
- `min_engine_version` = lowest game version the pack needs. 3-integer array in the manifest form `[1, RELEASE, PATCH]`: game 26.60 = `[1, 26, 60]`, NEVER `[26, 60, 0]` (Minecraft rejects it and caps at the highest released version). Real looked-up value, NEVER `[0,0,0]`. NEVER hardcode a minimum in docs; a documented minimum uses the same form (`1.26.60`).
- Manifest `format_version` = 2. Item/block `format_version` NEVER newer than `min_engine_version`.
- Compatibility line states only a verified minimum. NEVER "or newer" unverified.
- Updating old pack: ALWAYS keep UUIDs, persistent ids, working behavior. Upgrade deps deliberately. Raising `min_engine_version` drops players: say so in CHANGELOG.

Achievements/experiments (every new pack, meaningful update):
- ALWAYS inspect manifests, deps, stable-vs-preview imports, JSON, components, scripts, functions, world settings. Confirm experiment needs against current docs, NEVER keyword match. Stable Script API alone != experiment or achievements disabled.
- Pack files NEVER establish an existing world's settings.
- Nothing found: write "No experiment requirement detected in the inspected files."
- Prefer stable implementation. NEVER silently weaken a feature to dodge an experiment.
- Status: EVERY pack (add-on, map, script, minigame) = ALWAYS `Yes, achievement-friendly.` Every manifest carries `metadata.product_type` = `"addon"`, and the owner confirmed in game (ALN Pointer V20 `.mcaddon`) that a pack of that type keeps achievements allowed. Keep any cheat/command/world caveat as a following sentence. NEVER write Likely, Not or Unknown.
- Admin commands (`give_controller`, `end_game`, setup functions) = optional recovery tools. Cheats disable achievements in that world: say so wherever documented.
- Handoff: achievement status, experiments (none/required name/unknown), evidence, scope.

Implementation:
1. ALWAYS inspect manifest, entry points, imports, structure first.
2. Confirm each API member exists in the chosen stable module version.
3. Prefer event-driven logic. NEVER per-tick polling, broad entity searches, expensive block scans without need.
4. Vanilla JSON components and `.mcfunction` when simpler. Script API when needed.
5. Fix obvious malformed files.
6. Handoff records: target game version, `min_engine_version`, module versions, preview/experimental needs, references + date, verification status (source created | package built | in-game tested, NEVER mixed).

### 1.3 Version Format and Manifests
**Manifest versioning (Bedrock engine format):** `header.version` MUST be a 3-integer array `[X, Y, Z]` (e.g. `[1, 0, 0]`, `[2, 0, 0]`); `metadata.version` MUST be the string `"X.Y.Z"` (e.g. `"1.0.0"`). The Bedrock engine rejects non-array values or strings like `"vX"` in manifests. NEVER use `vX` syntax inside any `manifest.json` field.
**Per-pack user-facing format:** Every user-visible reference to a version uses `vX` (a single whole number): pack title suffix `(vX)`, changelog headings `## [vX]`, package filenames `(Shortcut VX.ext)`, README download links, CurseForge/MCPEDL release names, snapshot folder prefix `V`. The two formats are strictly separate: manifests = `[X, 0, 0]` / `"X.0.0"`, everything the user sees = `vX`.
- Version = `vX` format (single whole number, e.g. `v2`, `v3`; Bedrock `header.version` maps to `[X, 0, 0]`; legacy `X.Y.Z` supported). NEVER `X.Y.Z.A`. No fourth part, no Alpha. Same version in `metadata.version`, `(vX)` titles, changelog headings, package names, README links, snapshots.

- Update: X+1 (v1 -> v2, v2 -> v3; legacy 1.2.3 converts to v2). Manifest title suffix equals version (e.g. `(v2)`).
- Manifest title = `Add-on Name (vX)` (e.g. `Vanilla Quality of Life (v2)`). Exactly one terminal suffix, equals `header.version` (or major integer), BP/RP match. NEVER duplicate suffixes. `scripts/validate_manifest_titles.py [--project <path>]` fails the job on violation (also notes non-fail: format_version != 2, missing/zero `min_engine_version`, missing `metadata.authors`).

- `header.description` contains `by ALNStudios` exactly once. NEVER attribution in title.
- NEVER change pack UUIDs or world storage keys to rename a title. NEVER regenerate header UUIDs (breaks worlds).
- Build sync: every processed project aligns all source manifests to one highest `X.Y.Z`: `header.version`, `metadata.version`, title suffix, module versions, same-project BP/RP deps. FORCES `metadata.authors` = `["ALNStudios"]` and `metadata.product_type` = `"addon"` in EVERY BP and RP manifest (new packs, updates, builds; NEVER another author or type), optional `metadata.license`. `product_type` `"addon"` is what keeps achievements allowed (owner-confirmed in game). The Bedrock Wiki says Mojang removed this in game 26.50 (MCPE-242529): if a newer game version shows achievements disabled, report it, do not hide it.
- Snapshots: `builds/<pack>/versions/V<X.Y.Z>/` (packages, `source.zip`, `source.sha256`, `build_info.json`). Refreshed when source/packages differ. Last 5 kept.
- NOTHING auto-bumps. The new version is written in CHANGELOG.md AND, in the same update script, into every source manifest with `python3 scripts/manifest_tools.py --project <path> --set vX` (header.version, metadata.version, title suffix, module and dependency versions; UUIDs untouched; never lowers)
, so every update changes the build. Publish to CurseForge uploads the newest changelog version as written and groups every entry since the last publish. The passive build (`Build Changed Packs & Sync Catalogs`) aligns each pack to its highest existing version and rebuilds. `--bump` / `AUTO_BUMP_MANIFEST=true` remain in `scripts/auto_bump_and_build.py` for manual use only.
- Package name: **`(<Shortcut> VX.<ext>)`** (example **`(MiningHelmet V1.mcaddon)`**, or legacy **`(<Shortcut> VX.Y.Z.<ext>)`**). Always format name references in bold with parentheses. Shortcut = short, letters and digits only (NEVER `-`, `_`, spaces, `by ALNStudios`), close to the add-on name, kept in `scripts/pack_shortcuts.json` by project folder (fallback: folder name joined). Custom via `metadata.package_filename` or `PACK_FILENAME_TEMPLATE`; MUST contain `V{version}` and no parenthesis in the file name itself, else the default is used. CurseForge upload copy uses the same name. `python3 scripts/rename_packages.py --check|--apply` renames old packages and repairs references.
- Creator name `ALNStudios`. `python3 scripts/rename_brand.py --apply` migrates old name. NEVER rename lowercase namespaces (`allenscreations:`).
- Tools: `python3 scripts/manifest_tools.py --project <path> --check|--align|--bump` (`--all --check`). `sync_manifest_versions.py`: deps and `min_engine_version` maintenance; set `min_engine_version` by hand after lookup. `sync_project_docs.py` NOT in build: edit DESCRIPTION/CHANGELOG deliberately. `sync_readme_links.py [--check]` repairs README links (runs after every build).
- LOCK: every `MCPEDL/Addons/` project has `LOCK` at root starting `ALNStudios Add-on Lock v1`. Importer creates it, build enforces it. Missing/invalid = release-blocking: report path, NEVER call build-ready. Safeguard only, not encryption.

### 1.4 Recipe Unlock
- ALWAYS unlock every custom crafting recipe by default. Shaped = object `"unlock": { "context": "AlwaysUnlocked" }`. Shapeless = array `"unlock": [{ "context": "AlwaysUnlocked" }]`. NEVER mix forms in one recipe. Validators accept either; generator writes these.
- Covers `minecraft:recipe_shaped`, `minecraft:recipe_shapeless` only. NEVER add `unlock` to furnace/brewing/smithing/other unless Samples shows support.
- Validators parse JSON and check `unlock` key. NEVER text search for `AlwaysUnlocked`.
- NEVER change ingredients, result, count, station/tags, shaped/shapeless structure. NEVER rely on recipe-book discovery unless asked.
- Applies to all new recipes and any recipe touched. Check type against Samples first.

### 1.5 Recipe Images, Sprites, and Platform Descriptions
- ALWAYS: every custom crafting recipe gets a diagram and custom items get sprites. The build makes diagrams and sprites with `scripts/generate_recipe_images.py --all --all-items` (PNGs in `<pack>/assets/recipes/` and `<pack>/assets/items/`, sprites 128x128 nearest-neighbor for all new items, not just craftable ones); agents NEVER run it, they keep the recipe JSON, item JSONs, and add-on textures correct. `<pack>/assets/snapshots/` holds screenshots and GIFs the user uploads: NEVER rename, edit or delete. Snapshots display clean images without file-name text captions in galleries. `scripts/lib/build_description_views.py` shows all three folders automatically in responsive galleries (`Custom Items`, `Crafting Recipes`, `Screenshots`) on the CurseForge page; NEVER manually insert raw HTML `<img>` tags for asset files into `DESCRIPTION.md` (which blocks the automated gallery renderer from displaying them). Commit the images in the same task. ONLY for DESCRIPTION. AI image generation prompts for banners and logos belong solely in the generated Listing Kit (`Package/<Name>.listing.md`) under AI Image Prompts; NEVER use AI image generation for recipes or item sprites unless asked.
- ALWAYS verified absolute raw GitHub URLs. Confirm image exists, committed, matches item/recipe. Remove outdated.
- Sprites: real texture from add-on or authoritative vanilla source. Inventory-style, NEVER cropped world textures. Block-only: build small isometric cube from verified top+side faces, NEVER stretch one face.
- Build diagrams by code from recipe JSON + add-on result texture + MCicons icons only (no icon for an id: report it, never substitute). Recipe JSON authoritative. NEVER change a recipe to fit an image.
- Board: ONLY `scripts/CRAFTING_TABLE.svg` via `scripts/lib/crafting_template.py` (ids `slot-0`..`slot-8` row-major, `slot-N-fill`, `output`, `output-fill`, `arrow`). NEVER `CRAFTINGBOARD.PNG`, screenshots, hand-drawn boards. SVG missing: stop, report.
- ALWAYS full 3x3. Shaped keeps exact placement + empties. Shapeless fills row-major. NEVER flatten to list. Show quantities and output count. Colors from the SVG, background NEVER transparent. Vanilla gray UI, crisp nearest-neighbor, no frames/gradients/glow/labels/emojis. One diagram per row at 400x222.
- Minecraft icons (recipe diagrams, vanilla item sprites, README and listing images, anything showing a vanilla item or block): the reference is MCicons, the release file `reference/mcicons/mcicons-<ver>.tgz` (`package/dist/icons/minecraft_<id>.png`, 64x64; `scripts/lib/mcicons.py` reads it, `scripts/sync_mcicons.py` downloads it, the build refreshes it). Use the icon from that file, NEVER another icon site. Keep its `ASSET-NOTICE.md` with any redistributed icon (Minecraft art belongs to Mojang Studios / Microsoft).
- Icon source: MCicons ONLY, no fallback. An id without an icon (custom id, newer item) is reported, never replaced by a Samples or Wiki texture, another pack, a guessed name or an approximation. Samples stays the source for ids, recipes, components and loot tables, not for icons.
- Wiki images: CC BY-NC-SA, conflicts with monetized pages. ONLY with explicit user approval of that image. Names `Invicon_<Title_Case_Name>.png`, `ItemSprite_<lowercase-hyphenated-name>.png`. Open file page, confirm license. Record file page URL, license, date in generator header comment or `scripts/ASSET_SOURCES.md`; add attribution to DESCRIPTION. NEVER bulk-copy. Wiki blocked: report, fall back to Samples.
- ALWAYS map every ingredient to a texture first (filenames differ from ids; `textures/items/` or `textures/blocks/`; verify path + item). NEVER substitute or skip. Report all unresolved together, stop.
- Optional per-pack listing images (distinct from the profile banners in 2.3; user supplies both): clickable in Creator and Permissions, 16:9 JPEG `assets/Images/<slug>-curseforge.jpg`, `<slug>-mcpedl.jpg`, link to verified pack listing. NEVER placeholders or invented URLs. Pair only if both images and both URLs exist.
- Generators: `scripts/`, fail clearly on missing Samples/texture/recipe. Register in `.github/workflows/build-and-sync.yml` (run step in the recipe images step, `git add` in the commit step) and mirror the same command in the manual fallback `.github/workflows/generate-recipe-images.yml`. Workflow commits PNGs `[skip ci]`. NEVER call images published until workflow succeeded and PNGs exist.
- Technical reference: Bedrock Wiki explains; verify schema/ids/format version against Samples + current docs. Cite it as community docs; link, NEVER copy.

### 1.6 Function Files
Paths from repo root. A function file existing != it runs.
- Functions: `<project>/behavior_pack/functions/<name>.mcfunction`. Tick: `functions/tick.json` `values` = names without suffix (`oneblock_tick`).
- Before delivery: 1 inspect real manifest, functions, `tick.json`, install steps, report paths; 2 `tick.json` valid, right dir, names existing function; 3 check syntax, ids, coordinates, deps (scoreboards). NEVER make a score-dependent command the only initial-block path unless init guaranteed; 4 validate JSON and `node --check` JS directly (project scripts run in the `.sh`), state what passed; 5 custom item delivered but block missing: diagnose item delivery and function execution separately (pack activation, world links, tick registration, name/path, command errors) BEFORE fallbacks or version bump.
- NEVER claim `tick.json` missing without inspecting installed/packaged copy.

### 1.7 OneBlock, Maps, Scripts, Minigames
General:
- OneBlock script = general pack. MUST work in any world. NEVER depend on a world's terrain/database/settings. Project-specific (spawn, cleanup, shop) lives in that project's spec (Dirtbound: `SPEC.md` in its folder).
- Standard map coords (EVERY map, ALWAYS): OneBlock core `(0,64,0)`, Bedrock under core `(0,63,0)`, spawn `(0,65,0)`. NEVER deviate unless the user explicitly overrides.
- `Mcpeworlds/{Creative,Survival,Hardcore}/` = ONLY world foundations (convenience targets, not a usage limit). NEVER old world ZIPs, `Oneblock-assets.mcworld`, `assets/Void_For_Workv2.mcworld`, persisted `assets/oneblock-template/`.
- NEVER create/fabricate/borrow/require `db/CURRENT` or LevelDB data (database-free void design). NEVER modify source foundations; build renames only the staged copy's world name.
- Build: `build-and-sync.yml` (OneBlock steps) copies each foundation to staging, keeps its `level.dat`, adds map BP + RP, packages `.mcworld` into `MCPEDL/Maps/<Map-Name>/`. Source: `MCPEDL/Maps/<Map-Name>/behavior_pack/`, `resource_pack/`.
- Maps ship Creative+Survival+Hardcore worlds. Add-ons/Scripts/Minigames ship no world: the Test World was removed. NEVER `test_world.json`, `<Name> - Test.mcworld`, a separate tester package or `Tester Build/`. Add-on workflows NEVER misclassify a Map.
- One OneBlock gameplay pack per world (maps only). Installing a different one: remove the old pack folders + link entries from all 3 modes first. NEVER touch unrelated packs.
- World lock: the build locks the settings of Survival and Hardcore (`level.dat` flags `isFromLockedTemplate`, `hasLockedBehaviorPack`, and `hasLockedResourcePack` when the map has a resource pack). Creative stays unlocked. `immutableWorld` is NEVER set (the world would become read-only). Override per map with `"lock_settings": ["Survival"]` in `oneblock.json` (a list, may be empty). The effect of these flags is not in the Bedrock docs: mark it "not tested in game" until the owner opens the locked worlds.
- Install per mode (identical in all three): `behavior_pack/` -> `<mode>/behavior_packs/<Map-Name>_BP/`, `resource_pack/` -> `<mode>/resource_packs/<Map-Name>_RP/`. Update = replace whole folder. Link files at world root `world_behavior_packs.json` / `world_resource_packs.json`: `[{ "pack_id": "<manifest header uuid>", "version": [1, 0, 0] }]`, replace same `pack_id` on update. NEVER invent UUID/version. Unresolved deps: stop, report path.
- Map README: download-focused. Link only compiled `.mcworld` that exist. NEVER claim an archive matches source unless checked. Same for `MCPEDL/Maps/README.md`.

Payload import:
- `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is the generator file. Agent asks type (addon|map|script|minigame), conducts ~90% research on latest stable Bedrock APIs, presents the Plan for user confirmation, and returns single-line base64 ZIP made by running code, NEVER by hand. `project.json` = `{"type": "...", "name": "..."}`.
- `create-project-from-base64.yml`: folder name + base64 (or `.b64` under `MCPEDL/incoming/`) -> `scripts/import_project_payload.py` -> imports `scripts/build_payload.py` (same checks agent-side and repo-side).
- Destinations: addon `MCPEDL/Addons/<Name>/` (`.mcaddon`, importer writes `LOCK`); map `MCPEDL/Maps/<Name>/` (`.mcworld`, importer writes `oneblock.json`, installs `scripts/lib/Oneblock_Progression.js`, builds 3 worlds); script `MCPEDL/Script/<Name>/` (`.mcpack`, no Controller); minigame `MCPEDL/Minigame/<Name>/` (`.mcaddon`, Controller).
- Importer creates `assets/snapshots/`, `assets/recipes/` and `assets/items/` (each with `.gitkeep`) in every imported project when missing, never touching existing files. Payloads NEVER ship `assets/`.
- Importer validates BEFORE writing. Rejects unsafe paths, symlinks, world data (`db/`, `level.dat`), nested archives, shipped link files, disallowed types, bad manifests, recipes without unlock, per-type misses. Icons MUST match repo copies in `Mcpeworlds/assets/resources_pack/textures/items/` byte for byte. Payload size capped; duplicate/case-colliding ZIP entries and control chars rejected; folder names differing only by case or existing under another type refused unless `overwrite` (lists deleted files); count actual bytes while extracting (declared ZIP sizes can be forged).
- Map requires: `tick.json` lists `void_cleaner` first; `void_cleaner.mcfunction` starts with the Y=-64 fill line; `oneblock_setup.mcfunction` exists; Trader Ledger item + texture entry + `trader_ledger.png`. Others need no void cleaner/ledger.
- Folder name typed per run. NEVER hardcode. Payload with only `oneblock.json` still = map.
- Sections 1.3, 1.4, 1.6, 1.7 change: ALWAYS update `PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md`, `scripts/build_payload.py`, importer in the same change, then `scripts/selftest_project_types.py`. This file wins on conflict.

Scripts and minigames:
- Script project `MCPEDL/Script/<Name>/`: script BP, `.mcpack`. Minigame `MCPEDL/Minigame/<Name>/`: BP + RP, `.mcaddon`.
- Every Minigame ships Controller (`aln:<minigame_name>_controller`, `controller.png` 16x16 at `Mcpeworlds/assets/resources_pack/textures/items/controller.png`). Opens the ALN UI Redesign main menu (1.14): exactly 3 buttons, **Start**, **About**, **Exit**; while running the first button = **End Game**. The importer installs the skin font, `pixelify.js` and the About icon into the minigame. State (idle/running/ended) in a world dynamic property. NEVER opt out.
- Only host/operator gets and uses it. Start works while idle. End Game asks confirm, calls `endGame()`, needs no cheats. Start opens the How to play page (Start Game + Back) before the game begins. About page has creator, version, one line on the pack, profiles and a Back button. Game ends via win/lose logic calling `endGame()`.
- Controller KEPT while game runs. `consumeOnStart: true` in `registerController` restores old behavior (Start consumes first Controller unless Creative; End Game only reachable by host still carrying one). Host/operator gets one on every spawn (incl. death) when carrying none; game end gives a fresh one.
- Stuck state: NEVER stay `running` forever. On world load, a script that cannot restore a live game resets to `idle` and gives Controller. `give_controller.mcfunction`, `end_game.mcfunction` ship with every Controller minigame but need cheats: last resort, NEVER only recovery.
- No test world (removed, NEVER recreate). The importer rejects a payload that ships `test_world.json`. Experiments/achievement state of a world come from the foundation, unverified.
- Verify: manifest, `tick.json`, functions wired (1.6); installed pack matches source per mode; link entry matches manifest UUID+version; RP dependency resolves; script syntax checks pass. Importer, `build_oneblock_variants_from_template.py` (`verify_archive`), `scripts/selftest_project_types.py` run these. State exactly what passed. Structure checks NEVER prove Minecraft loaded the world.

Project specs live in the project folder (Dirtbound: `MCPEDL/Maps/OneBlock-Dirtbound-Kingdom-Beta/SPEC.md`).

### 1.8 Workflows
- Workflows: `.github/workflows/`. ALWAYS read the actual file before editing or describing it. NEVER claim a run succeeded unless checked. NEVER trust summaries of workflows (they drift).
- Retired, NEVER recreate or reference: `update-min-version.yml`, `scripts/update_min_version.py`, `create-addon-scaffold.yml`, `test-crafting-template.yml`, `create-mcpe-world.yml`, `scripts/create_mcpe_world.py`, `scripts/check_generator_sync.py`, `scripts/build_test_world.py`, `test_world.json`, `<Name> - Test.mcworld`.
- Version rules (1.3) changing workflow behavior: update the workflow in the same change.
- Entry points: read the workflow file; never rely on a summary of it.
- Helper scripts: `scripts/` (read each script for options). `scripts/check_instruction_files.py` fails CI when an instruction file breaks size/format rules.

### 1.9 Repository Layout
```
MCPE-Curseforges/
├── INSTRUCTIONS.md  PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md  README.md
├── scripts/  .github/workflows/  tests/
├── Mcpeworlds/{Creative,Survival,Hardcore}/
├── MCPEDL/{Addons,Maps,Script,Minigame}/<Name>/  MCPEDL/incoming/
└── builds/<Name>/ build_info.json  versions/V<X.Y.Z>/
```

### 1.10 Review and Security
AI payloads = untrusted until a person reviews. Human PR review is the real safeguard; checks reduce risk, NEVER prove safety.
- ALWAYS review `behavior_pack/scripts/` and every `.mcfunction` in the PR before merge. `direct` commit only for a payload you made and checked. PR mode needs "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests".
- `build_payload.py` enforces: imports only `@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, `@minecraft/vanilla-data`, relative files; `eval`/`Function` rejected; every `.js` passes `node --check`. Limits: eval test is a text pattern (misses `globalThis["ev"+"al"]`, aliasing, `import(variable)`). Reviewers MUST read scripts.
- UUIDs unique across `MCPEDL/` (own folder excluded). `min_engine_version` = real looked-up version.
- NEVER `${{ inputs.* }}` or step outputs inside `run:`. Pass via `env:`, quote.
- Docs MUST pass `build_payload.py` `DOC:` checks (failures, not warnings).

### 1.11 Known Untested Assumptions
Open until confirmed in game. NEVER state as fact in docs.
1. Void cleaner: Y=-64 fill succeeds for a player spawning in a fresh void world; conditional tag (Dirtbound `SPEC.md`) retries correctly.
2. World host reports as Operator via `playerPermissionLevel` with cheats off (Controller reaches host on achievement-eligible world).
3. `isOp()` absent in 2.x `@minecraft/server` (Controller uses a helper). Re-check on every module bump.
4. Controller behavior across rejoin, death, reload. Tested only with mocked modules.
5. Shipped functions and Controller do not disable achievements unless an admin command is run.
6. Experiment/achievement state of each `Mcpeworlds/` foundation.

### 1.12 Vanilla Limits and Workarounds
Applies to every addon, map, script, minigame, item, entity, block, trade, recipe and enchantment, new or updated.
- A hardcoded vanilla limit blocks the requested mechanic (examples: mutually exclusive enchantments, item, entity or component caps, trade or recipe limits) AND the standard approach (behavior JSON, commands, crafting, enchanting) fails: NEVER drop the feature silently. The standard approach works: use it, no workaround.
- Lightest workaround first. 1) Data-driven: custom item, block, entity, recipe, loot, trade, spawn rule, animation controller, tag, component. 2) `@minecraft/server`: custom components, events, ItemStack and Entity APIs, dynamic properties, tags or scoreboard for state. 3) Structural: helper or proxy entity, swapped or replaced item or entity, tiered item variants, `.mcstructure` or template placement, function-driven effects.
- NEVER invent an API, component, event or behavior. Use only what the looked-up stable version (1.2) provides and the repo scripts accept. Cannot verify: say so in DESCRIPTION.md.
- Modular: one small file per workaround with one entry point (script: `behavior_pack/scripts/lib/<name>.js` imported relatively; data: its own function file, entity or item definition). Reuse an existing module. NEVER copy the logic per feature.
- Safe: idempotent setup, caps and cleanup for spawned entities and loops, nothing that corrupts worlds or other packs.
- Conflict Prevention & Isolation:
  1. Custom namespace isolation: Always prefix custom items, blocks, and entities with `aln:<name>` (e.g. `aln:<minigame_name>_controller`).
  2. Avoid Vanilla Entity Overrides: Overriding vanilla entity JSON files (e.g. `entities/zombie.json`) completely wipes out other addons modifying the same entity in Bedrock's load order. Whenever possible, hook into vanilla entity mechanics using `@minecraft/server` Script API event subscriptions (`world.afterEvents.entityDie`, `playerInteractWithEntity`, `entityHurt`, etc.) rather than replacing the vanilla entity JSON.
- Structural NBT & .mcstructure: When a mechanic requires pre-built templates or saved blocks/entities, place `.mcstructure` and `.nbt` files strictly under `behavior_pack/structures/` (or an optional namespace subfolder like `behavior_pack/structures/aln/<name>.mcstructure`). These files must be generated only by code or exported from Bedrock tools (never hand-written arbitrary bytes). Shipped files must contain no comments, obey the 2 MB per-file size cap, and be stated as "not tested in game" until verified in Minecraft.

- Docs (`DOCS_RULES.md`): player-facing result only, no code or API names; the trade-off under Good To Know. State achievement and experiment impact per 1.2.

### 1.13 ALN UI Redesign (UI skin)
- `assets/aln-ui-redesign/` is the shared UI skin (formerly Cheatstick UI): `README.txt` (how to apply), `design.json` (tokens), `textures/`, `preview_reference.png`.
- Any pack that adds or changes a menu, form, HUD or screen reads `README.txt` and `design.json` first and ships the skin in its Resource Pack: textures copied in (never linked), tokens reused (NEVER invent colors or layout). Version the Resource Pack with `manifest_tools.py --set` like every update.
- Pack task needs the skin: ask for Collect Files `paths` = `assets/aln-ui-redesign, <pack folder>/resource_pack`. NEVER edit the shared folder from a pack task unless asked.
- Menu text uses the shared Pixelify Sans glyph font in `assets/aln-ui-redesign/font/` (its README.txt has the steps): copy the glyph sheet into the Resource Pack `font/`, wrap strings with `pixelify()`.

### 1.14 ALNStudios Redesigned UI Standard (every add-on with a menu)
Placeholders `<PackName>`, `<MainAction>`, `<version>`. NEVER hardcode one pack.
- Main menu: ONE ActionForm, exactly 3 buttons: `<MainAction>`, About, Exit. X also exits. Menus loop; every sub-screen has Back.
- Title `§l§6@ALNStudios §e<PackName>§r (v<version>)` = the ONLY colored form text. Script version constant == manifest version, bumped together.
- Colors ONLY in title and action bar. Body, labels, slider labels, buttons, summaries: default color + `§l`, `§r` after bold. NEVER `§f §7 §e §a` etc. in forms.
- About: title `About ALNStudios`. Body: creator, `<PackName>` + version, one line on the pack, CurseForge and MCPEDL profile text (plain, not clickable), not-affiliated-with-Mojang line. One Back button to main menu. Icon = `assets/aln-ui-redesign/about_icon.png` shipped in the RP as `textures/ui/aln/about_icon` (no `.png`); RP or file missing: `textures/items/name_tag`.
- Icons: `<MainAction>` icon matches the pack, only if the pack needs one and a verified path exists (else none). About and Exit always have an icon. ONLY paths verified in Mojang `resource_pack/textures/item_texture.json` (bedrock-samples). NEVER guess; no verified path = no icon. `wooden_` -> `wood_`, `golden_` -> `gold_`. Spears `textures/items/spear/<wood|stone|copper|iron|gold|diamond|netherite>_spear`. Nautilus armor `textures/items/nautilus_armor/<material>_nautilus_armor`.
- Feedback: `player.onScreenDisplay.setActionBar`, NEVER chat. Green success, yellow warning, red error. Second message chained with `system.runTimeout(..., 50)`.
- Slider/modal forms: header = item or context name, label = current state, divider after every control. Optional form methods go through a guarded helper (a missing method NEVER crashes). Read `formValues` filtering numbers.
- NEVER slide or fade animations in UI JSON. Use the skin in `assets/aln-ui-redesign/` (1.13).

### 1.15 Custom Sounds
- Audio files cannot travel inside a spec. A project that needs custom sounds keeps a pure-Python synthesizer at `<project>/sound_sources/make_sounds.py` (reference: `MCPEDL/Addons/ALNSpiritSphere/sound_sources/make_sounds.py`). It writes `.ogg` files into `<project>/resource_pack/sounds/` and needs ffmpeg (Termux: `pkg install ffmpeg`).
- An update that adds sounds uses `@@ create` for `make_sounds.py` and `resource_pack/sounds/sound_definitions.json` (`format_version` 1.14.0, one event per sound, sound paths without extension, category neutral). After the update script runs, the user runs: `python3 <project>/sound_sources/make_sounds.py`, then `git add`, `commit`, `push`.
- Sound event ids use the pattern `aln.<project>.<element>.<kind>` and are played from scripts with `playSound`. NEVER guess vanilla sound ids; use only ids verified through Context7.
- New sound files need a full Minecraft client restart to load. Say so in the handoff.
- `.ogg` is the only audio type allowed. `DESCRIPTION.md` must describe sounds in player terms and never name the script, ffmpeg or file names.

### 1.16 Bedrock Developer Role and Trusted Sources
- Role: expert Minecraft Bedrock Add-on and Script API developer. Build and maintain complete Behavior Packs, Resource Packs and Script API modules. Official NPM packages first (`@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, `@minecraft/math`); on any error, deprecated API or version mismatch verify through the Context7 libraries below (1.17).
- Structure: valid `manifest.json` (1.3), fresh v4 UUIDs for new packs only (NEVER regenerate existing ones), accurate module dependencies, clean JavaScript. NEVER ship TypeScript (`build_payload.py` allows only `.js`). `@minecraft/math` is NOT in `ALLOWED_MODULES` of `build_payload.py` (1.10): NEVER import or depend on it in a payload until the allow-list lists it; use plain JavaScript vector math and say so in the handoff.
- Assets: binary PNGs live in the Resource Pack `textures/` folder, registered by JSON (`textures/item_texture.json`, `textures/terrain_texture.json`), NEVER mixed with the registration files. Recipes reference vanilla item identifiers directly. NEVER duplicate vanilla PNGs unless needed as templates.
- Trusted sources (rely strictly and exclusively on these four Context7 libraries, queried per 1.17; NEVER invent API methods, outdated syntax or item ids): `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator` (Script API, `@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, JSON schemas, versions); `/mojang/bedrock-samples` (vanilla textures, recipes, entities, definitions, formats); `/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev` (manifest structure, techniques); `/allenscreations/mcpe-curseforges` (this repo's rules and scripts). No other library, site or package page is a source. Priority and disagreements follow 1.1: report, NEVER choose silently.
- Failure (script does not load, texture does not render, syntax changed in a newer update): query the Context7 libraries above (1.17) for the exact updated syntax, manifest dependency versions, valid vanilla identifiers and working patterns BEFORE rewriting code.
- Output: complete ready-to-use files with exact repo-root-relative paths, delivered ONLY as the single update `.sh` (`<Repofilename>_<Version>_<Rand>.sh`) or the single pack `.sh` (new packs) and reply format above (Reply Format, Termux Script Rules).
- Item/Block menu_category: When defining `menu_category` in custom items or blocks, `group` MUST start with a namespace (e.g. `minecraft:itemGroup.name.<group>` or `<pack_namespace>:itemGroup.name.<group>`). Unnamespaced group strings (such as `itemGroup.name.helmet`) fail Bedrock parsing (`Failed to parse field ' -> description -> menu_category -> group: string must be prefixed with a namespace (eg. namespace:value)'`). When no specific vanilla group applies, omit `group` and specify only `category` (`items`, `equipment`, `construction`, `nature`).

### 1.17 Documentation Lookup (Context7 MCP)

Docs, API, vanilla data and repo files are read with the Context7 MCP tool `query-docs`. NEVER by pasted URL, NEVER from memory. Applies to every addon, map, script, minigame, update and docs task.
- Libraries: ONLY these three, IDs passed exactly. NEVER call `resolve-library-id`, NEVER query any other library.
  - `/mojang/bedrock-samples`: vanilla ids, textures, recipes, components, entities, formats, `version.json` (1.1 priority 2).
  - `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator`: `@minecraft/server` and `@minecraft/server-ui` API, JSON schemas, stable and module versions (1.1 priority 3).
  - `/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev`: manifest structure, scripting guides, techniques (1.1 priority 5).
- Before ANY code, manifest, JSON, `.mcfunction`, recipe, version or doc fact: query first, write second. Batch: at most ONE `query-docs` call per needed `libraryId` (several topics together in that call). NEVER several calls to the same `libraryId`, NEVER sequential retry loops. Name the exact member, file, id or schema and the module or game version (e.g. `ItemStack constructor @minecraft/server`, `recipe_shaped format`). All three calls may go in one parallel turn.
- Verify every import, class, method, property, event, enum, component and JSON field against the retrieved text before using it. NEVER guess. NEVER outdated syntax: the `mojang-minecraft` modules, `world.events`, 1.x-era imports.
- Empty or doubtful result: do NOT query again. Say "not verified" (1.2), NEVER fill the gap from memory. Results that disagree with each other: report per 1.1.
- Context7 is an index and can lag the game. Versions come from the retrieved text only, with the query date. A result that does not state the latest stable version = "not verified". Preview/beta members only as 1.2 allows.
- Repo files: read from the kit zip (`New-Pack.zip`, `Maintenance.zip` or the pack's review zip) or from the repo clone — these always beat any external index. Scripts that must run (`build_payload.py`, `doc_template.py`, `import_project_payload.py`, `make_pack_script.py`, `pixel_json_to_png.py`) need their COMPLETE unchanged text from the zip under `files/`. Incomplete: ask the user to attach the zip. NEVER rewrite from memory.
- Handoff: one bullet `Docs queried (YYYY-MM-DD): <library IDs and topics>`.
- NEVER put library IDs, query text or the word Context7 in shipped files or player-facing docs (2.2).

---

## Parts 2 and 3: Docs, Audit, References (moved)
`DOCS_RULES.md`: README, DESCRIPTION and CHANGELOG rules and templates (2.1 to 2.5); `/checkrm` audit procedure and the Minecraft Wiki and Bedrock Samples references (3.1 to 3.4).

- [ ] Versions per 1.3, script name per 1.1.
- [ ] CHANGELOG.md updated, top heading == new version == script name version.
- [ ] Docs match the `DOCS_RULES.md` templates exactly, no placeholders, no unverified claims.
- [ ] No comments in `.js`/`.mcfunction`/`.json`. Recipes unlocked (1.4).
- [ ] Changed JSON validated. Instruction file changed: `scripts/check_instruction_files.py` passes.
- [ ] Manifest versions use `[X, Y, Z]` / `"X.Y.Z"` format; per-pack titles/changelogs/packages use `vX` format (1.3).
````

## Policy Doc: PACK_GENERATOR_INSTRUCTIONS.md

````markdown
# Pack Generator Instructions (AI Agent, v42)



Builds a complete **addon**, **map**, **script**, or **minigame** from the user's idea. Returns ONE Termux `.sh` pack script that carries the whole project inside it (as base64). No other file is delivered.

Read `.agents/skills/mcpe-new-pack/SKILL.md` first (it is also `SKILL.md` at the root of the AI review zip), then this file and `DOCS_RULES.md` for a build task. Part 1 below is an excerpt of `INSTRUCTIONS.md`; the checker keeps its lines identical. Maintenance, workflows, audits, recipe images: `INSTRUCTIONS.md`. Numbers 1.x and 4.x refer to this file; 2.x to `DOCS_RULES.md`.

| Part | Covers |
| :--- | :--- |
| 1 (excerpt) | Accuracy, versions, manifests, recipe unlock, functions, security, untested assumptions |
| 2 | In `DOCS_RULES.md`: README, DESCRIPTION, CHANGELOG rules, templates |
| 4 | Generator: ask type, build, check, deliver, reply |

Precedence: Part 1 wins. `DOCS_RULES.md` follows Part 1. Part 4 follows both. `INSTRUCTIONS.md` wins on conflict. Accuracy and requested scope outrank any template. Script and file disagree: tell the user.
Order of work: read Part 1 (incl. 1.17), `DOCS_RULES.md`, then Part 4 from 4.0. Query Context7 per 1.17 before writing any code, manifest, JSON or doc fact. Unclear request: ask ONE line, then proceed. New pack creation: conduct mandatory ~90% research and present the Plan first (4.0a); wait for user confirmation before building.

---

## Part 1 (excerpt): Core Policy

### 1.1 Accuracy
- ALWAYS inspect source files before claims. Ask only if an unresolved choice affects gameplay, compatibility, distribution, data loss, or versioning. Else use defaults.
- NEVER invent features, versions, links, assets, test results, compatibility, achievement support, performance, platform behavior. Separate verified facts from assumptions.
- NEVER claim in-game test/function run/gameplay result unless tested in Minecraft. Archive/JSON/syntax checks prove structure only.
- Source priority (report disagreements, NEVER choose silently). Read sources 2, 3 and 5 through Context7 (1.17): 1 add-on's own files; 1b MCicons release file `reference/mcicons/mcicons-<ver>.tgz` (NOT in Context7; ids in `docs/mcicons-index.md`; the ONLY source of vanilla item and block icons, no fallback); 2 Mojang Bedrock Samples (`/mojang/bedrock-samples`; offline copy reference/bedrock-samples/bedrock-samples.min.json.gz): vanilla ids, textures, recipes, components, formats;
 3 Minecraft Creator Docs (`/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator`): stable versions, API; 4 Minecraft Wiki (https://minecraft.wiki/, not in Context7, only through a web tool when one exists): display names, edition, icon look; 5 Bedrock Wiki (`/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev`): formats, techniques. Sources 4-5 NEVER override 1-3 on ids, versions, API.
- NEVER comments in shipped `.js`, `.mcfunction`, `.json`. Explanations go in docs or handoff.
- NEVER imply Mojang/Microsoft endorsement.
- `.sh` names: `(Repofilename)_(Version)_(Rand).sh`. Repofilename = project/folder/file changed, no extension, no spaces (hyphens). Version = new `vX` (e.g. `v2`, or legacy `X.Y.Z`), instruction version (`v5`) or repo version (`v80`). Rand = >=5 random lowercase/digits, fresh each time (`head -c 200 /dev/urandom | tr -dc 'a-z0-9' | head -c 6`), NEVER reused. Example `Dirtbound_v2_k7q2x.sh`. Same name in reply and run command.


### 1.2 Versions and Compatibility
Latest stable:
- ALWAYS look up latest stable Bedrock release and Script API module versions at time of work. Record date. NEVER trust numbers in this file or memory.
- Refs: Context7 `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator` (Script API versioning, `@minecraft/server` and `@minecraft/server-ui` stable references, platform version guidance), queried per 1.17. Community wikis NEVER pick versions or confirm API.
- Pick highest stable module version supported by target game and every dependency (check peers). NEVER preview/beta/RC unless a requested feature needs it; then disclose in docs and handoff.
- Game version and module version are separate. NEVER infer one from the other.

Engine:
- `min_engine_version` = lowest game version the pack needs. 3-integer array in the manifest form `[1, RELEASE, PATCH]`: game 26.60 = `[1, 26, 60]`, NEVER `[26, 60, 0]` (Minecraft rejects it and caps at the highest released version). Real looked-up value, NEVER `[0,0,0]`. NEVER hardcode a minimum in docs; a documented minimum uses the same form (`1.26.60`).
- Manifest `format_version` = 2. Item/block `format_version` NEVER newer than `min_engine_version`.
- Compatibility line states only a verified minimum. NEVER "or newer" unverified.
- Updating old pack: ALWAYS keep UUIDs, persistent ids, working behavior. Upgrade deps deliberately. Raising `min_engine_version` drops players: say so in CHANGELOG.

Achievements/experiments (every new pack, meaningful update):
- ALWAYS inspect manifests, deps, stable-vs-preview imports, JSON, components, scripts, functions, world settings. Confirm experiment needs against current docs, NEVER keyword match. Stable Script API alone != experiment or achievements disabled.
- Pack files NEVER establish an existing world's settings.
- Nothing found: write "No experiment requirement detected in the inspected files."
- Prefer stable implementation. NEVER silently weaken a feature to dodge an experiment.
- Status: EVERY pack (add-on, map, script, minigame) = ALWAYS `Yes, achievement-friendly.` Every manifest carries `metadata.product_type` = `"addon"`, and the owner confirmed in game (ALN Pointer V20 `.mcaddon`) that a pack of that type keeps achievements allowed. Keep any cheat/command/world caveat as a following sentence. NEVER write Likely, Not or Unknown.
- Admin commands (`give_controller`, `end_game`, setup functions) = optional recovery tools. Cheats disable achievements in that world: say so wherever documented.
- Handoff: achievement status, experiments (none/required name/unknown), evidence, scope.

Implementation:
1. ALWAYS inspect manifest, entry points, imports, structure first.
2. Confirm each API member exists in the chosen stable module version.
3. Prefer event-driven logic. NEVER per-tick polling, broad entity searches, expensive block scans without need.
4. Vanilla JSON components and `.mcfunction` when simpler. Script API when needed.
5. Fix obvious malformed files.
6. Handoff records: target game version, `min_engine_version`, module versions, preview/experimental needs, references + date, verification status (source created | package built | in-game tested, NEVER mixed).

### 1.3 Version Format and Manifests
**Manifest versioning (Bedrock engine format):** `header.version` MUST be a 3-integer array `[X, Y, Z]` (e.g. `[1, 0, 0]`, `[2, 0, 0]`); `metadata.version` MUST be the string `"X.Y.Z"` (e.g. `"1.0.0"`). The Bedrock engine rejects non-array values or strings like `"vX"` in manifests. NEVER use `vX` syntax inside any `manifest.json` field.
**Per-pack user-facing format:** Every user-visible reference to a version uses `vX` (a single whole number): pack title suffix `(vX)`, changelog headings `## [vX]`, package filenames `(Shortcut VX.ext)`, README download links, CurseForge/MCPEDL release names, snapshot folder prefix `V`. The two formats are strictly separate: manifests = `[X, 0, 0]` / `"X.0.0"`, everything the user sees = `vX`.
- Version = `vX` format (single whole number, e.g. `v2`, `v3`; Bedrock `header.version` maps to `[X, 0, 0]`; legacy `X.Y.Z` supported). NEVER `X.Y.Z.A`. No fourth part, no Alpha. Same version in `metadata.version`, `(vX)` titles, changelog headings, package names, README links, snapshots.

- Update: X+1 (v1 -> v2, v2 -> v3; legacy 1.2.3 converts to v2). Manifest title suffix equals version (e.g. `(v2)`).
- Manifest title = `Add-on Name (vX)` (e.g. `Vanilla Quality of Life (v2)`). Exactly one terminal suffix, equals `header.version` (or major integer), BP/RP match. NEVER duplicate suffixes. `scripts/validate_manifest_titles.py [--project <path>]` fails the job on violation (also notes non-fail: format_version != 2, missing/zero `min_engine_version`, missing `metadata.authors`).

- `header.description` contains `by ALNStudios` exactly once. NEVER attribution in title.
- NEVER change pack UUIDs or world storage keys to rename a title. NEVER regenerate header UUIDs (breaks worlds).
- Build sync: every processed project aligns all source manifests to one highest `X.Y.Z`: `header.version`, `metadata.version`, title suffix, module versions, same-project BP/RP deps. FORCES `metadata.authors` = `["ALNStudios"]` and `metadata.product_type` = `"addon"` in EVERY BP and RP manifest (new packs, updates, builds; NEVER another author or type), optional `metadata.license`. `product_type` `"addon"` is what keeps achievements allowed (owner-confirmed in game). The Bedrock Wiki says Mojang removed this in game 26.50 (MCPE-242529): if a newer game version shows achievements disabled, report it, do not hide it.
- Snapshots: `builds/<pack>/versions/V<X.Y.Z>/` (packages, `source.zip`, `source.sha256`, `build_info.json`). Refreshed when source/packages differ. Last 5 kept.
- NOTHING auto-bumps. The new version is written in CHANGELOG.md AND, in the same update script, into every source manifest with `python3 scripts/manifest_tools.py --project <path> --set vX` (header.version, metadata.version, title suffix, module and dependency versions; UUIDs untouched; never lowers)
, so every update changes the build. Publish to CurseForge uploads the newest changelog version as written and groups every entry since the last publish. The passive build (`Build Changed Packs & Sync Catalogs`) aligns each pack to its highest existing version and rebuilds. `--bump` / `AUTO_BUMP_MANIFEST=true` remain in `scripts/auto_bump_and_build.py` for manual use only.
- Package name: **`(<Shortcut> VX.<ext>)`** (example **`(MiningHelmet V1.mcaddon)`**, or legacy **`(<Shortcut> VX.Y.Z.<ext>)`**). Always format name references in bold with parentheses. Shortcut = short, letters and digits only (NEVER `-`, `_`, spaces, `by ALNStudios`), close to the add-on name, kept in `scripts/pack_shortcuts.json` by project folder (fallback: folder name joined). Custom via `metadata.package_filename` or `PACK_FILENAME_TEMPLATE`; MUST contain `V{version}` and no parenthesis in the file name itself, else the default is used. CurseForge upload copy uses the same name. `python3 scripts/rename_packages.py --check|--apply` renames old packages and repairs references.
- Creator name `ALNStudios`. `python3 scripts/rename_brand.py --apply` migrates old name. NEVER rename lowercase namespaces (`allenscreations:`).
- Tools: `python3 scripts/manifest_tools.py --project <path> --check|--align|--bump` (`--all --check`). `sync_manifest_versions.py`: deps and `min_engine_version` maintenance; set `min_engine_version` by hand after lookup. `sync_project_docs.py` NOT in build: edit DESCRIPTION/CHANGELOG deliberately. `sync_readme_links.py [--check]` repairs README links (runs after every build).
- LOCK: every `MCPEDL/Addons/` project has `LOCK` at root starting `ALNStudios Add-on Lock v1`. Importer creates it, build enforces it. Missing/invalid = release-blocking: report path, NEVER call build-ready. Safeguard only, not encryption.

### 1.4 Recipe Unlock
- ALWAYS unlock every custom crafting recipe by default. Shaped = object `"unlock": { "context": "AlwaysUnlocked" }`. Shapeless = array `"unlock": [{ "context": "AlwaysUnlocked" }]`. NEVER mix forms in one recipe. Validators accept either; generator writes these.
- Covers `minecraft:recipe_shaped`, `minecraft:recipe_shapeless` only. NEVER add `unlock` to furnace/brewing/smithing/other unless Samples shows support.
- Validators parse JSON and check `unlock` key. NEVER text search for `AlwaysUnlocked`.
- NEVER change ingredients, result, count, station/tags, shaped/shapeless structure. NEVER rely on recipe-book discovery unless asked.
- Applies to all new recipes and any recipe touched. Check type against Samples first.

### 1.6 Function Files
Paths from repo root. A function file existing != it runs.
- Functions: `<project>/behavior_pack/functions/<name>.mcfunction`. Tick: `functions/tick.json` `values` = names without suffix (`oneblock_tick`).
- Before delivery: 1 inspect real manifest, functions, `tick.json`, install steps, report paths; 2 `tick.json` valid, right dir, names existing function; 3 check syntax, ids, coordinates, deps (scoreboards). NEVER make a score-dependent command the only initial-block path unless init guaranteed; 4 validate JSON and `node --check` JS directly (project scripts run in the `.sh`), state what passed; 5 custom item delivered but block missing: diagnose item delivery and function execution separately (pack activation, world links, tick registration, name/path, command errors) BEFORE fallbacks or version bump.
- NEVER claim `tick.json` missing without inspecting installed/packaged copy.

### 1.10 Review and Security
AI payloads = untrusted until a person reviews. Human PR review is the real safeguard; checks reduce risk, NEVER prove safety.
- ALWAYS review `behavior_pack/scripts/` and every `.mcfunction` in the PR before merge. `direct` commit only for a payload you made and checked. PR mode needs "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests".
- `build_payload.py` enforces: imports only `@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, `@minecraft/vanilla-data`, relative files; `eval`/`Function` rejected; every `.js` passes `node --check`. Limits: eval test is a text pattern (misses `globalThis["ev"+"al"]`, aliasing, `import(variable)`). Reviewers MUST read scripts.
- UUIDs unique across `MCPEDL/` (own folder excluded). `min_engine_version` = real looked-up version.
- NEVER `${{ inputs.* }}` or step outputs inside `run:`. Pass via `env:`, quote.
- Docs MUST pass `build_payload.py` `DOC:` checks (failures, not warnings).

### 1.11 Known Untested Assumptions
Open until confirmed in game. NEVER state as fact in docs.
1. Void cleaner: Y=-64 fill succeeds for a player spawning in a fresh void world; conditional tag (Dirtbound `SPEC.md`) retries correctly.
2. World host reports as Operator via `playerPermissionLevel` with cheats off (Controller reaches host on achievement-eligible world).
3. `isOp()` absent in 2.x `@minecraft/server` (Controller uses a helper). Re-check on every module bump.
4. Controller behavior across rejoin, death, reload. Tested only with mocked modules.
5. Shipped functions and Controller do not disable achievements unless an admin command is run.
6. Experiment/achievement state of each `Mcpeworlds/` foundation.

### 1.16 Bedrock Developer Role and Trusted Sources
- Role: expert Minecraft Bedrock Add-on and Script API developer. Build and maintain complete Behavior Packs, Resource Packs and Script API modules. Official NPM packages first (`@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, `@minecraft/math`); on any error, deprecated API or version mismatch verify through the Context7 libraries below (1.17).
- Structure: valid `manifest.json` (1.3), fresh v4 UUIDs for new packs only (NEVER regenerate existing ones), accurate module dependencies, clean JavaScript. NEVER ship TypeScript (`build_payload.py` allows only `.js`). `@minecraft/math` is NOT in `ALLOWED_MODULES` of `build_payload.py` (1.10): NEVER import or depend on it in a payload until the allow-list lists it; use plain JavaScript vector math and say so in the handoff.
- Assets: binary PNGs live in the Resource Pack `textures/` folder, registered by JSON (`textures/item_texture.json`, `textures/terrain_texture.json`), NEVER mixed with the registration files. Recipes reference vanilla item identifiers directly. NEVER duplicate vanilla PNGs unless needed as templates.
- Trusted sources (rely strictly and exclusively on these four Context7 libraries, queried per 1.17; NEVER invent API methods, outdated syntax or item ids): `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator` (Script API, `@minecraft/server`, `@minecraft/server-ui`, JSON schemas, versions); `/mojang/bedrock-samples` (vanilla textures, recipes, entities, definitions, formats); `/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev` (manifest structure, techniques); `/allenscreations/mcpe-curseforges` (this repo's rules and scripts). No other library, site or package page is a source. Priority and disagreements follow 1.1: report, NEVER choose silently.
- Failure (script does not load, texture does not render, syntax changed in a newer update): query the Context7 libraries above (1.17) for the exact updated syntax, manifest dependency versions, valid vanilla identifiers and working patterns BEFORE rewriting code.
- Output: complete ready-to-use files with exact repo-root-relative paths, delivered ONLY as the single update `.sh` (`<Repofilename>_<Version>_<Rand>.sh`) or the single pack `.sh` (new packs) and reply format above (Reply Format, Termux Script Rules).

### 1.17 Documentation Lookup (Context7 MCP)
Docs, API, vanilla data and repo files are read with the Context7 MCP tool `query-docs`. NEVER by pasted URL, NEVER from memory. Applies to every addon, map, script, minigame, update and docs task.
- Libraries: ONLY these three, IDs passed exactly. NEVER call `resolve-library-id`, NEVER query any other library.
  - `/mojang/bedrock-samples`: vanilla ids, textures, recipes, components, entities, formats, `version.json` (1.1 priority 2).
  - `/microsoftdocs/minecraft-creator`: `@minecraft/server` and `@minecraft/server-ui` API, JSON schemas, stable and module versions (1.1 priority 3).
  - `/websites/wiki_bedrock_dev`: manifest structure, scripting guides, techniques (1.1 priority 5).
- Before ANY code, manifest, JSON, `.mcfunction`, recipe, version or doc fact: query first, write second. Batch: at most ONE `query-docs` call per needed `libraryId` (several topics together in that call). NEVER several calls to the same `libraryId`, NEVER sequential retry loops. Name the exact member, file, id or schema and the module or game version (e.g. `ItemStack constructor @minecraft/server`, `recipe_shaped format`). All three calls may go in one parallel turn.
- Verify every import, class, method, property, event, enum, component and JSON field against the retrieved text before using it. NEVER guess. NEVER outdated syntax: the `mojang-minecraft` modules, `world.events`, 1.x-era imports.
- Empty or doubtful result: do NOT query again. Say "not verified" (1.2), NEVER fill the gap from memory. Results that disagree with each other: report per 1.1.
- Context7 is an index and can lag the game. Versions come from the retrieved text only, with the query date. A result that does not state the latest stable version = "not verified". Preview/beta members only as 1.2 allows.
- Repo files: read from the kit zip (`New-Pack.zip`, `Maintenance.zip` or the pack's review zip) or from the repo clone — these always beat any external index. Scripts that must run (`build_payload.py`, `doc_template.py`, `import_project_payload.py`, `make_pack_script.py`, `pixel_json_to_png.py`) need their COMPLETE unchanged text from the zip under `files/`. Incomplete: ask the user to attach the zip. NEVER rewrite from memory.
- Handoff: one bullet `Docs queried (YYYY-MM-DD): <library IDs and topics>`.
- NEVER put library IDs, query text or the word Context7 in shipped files or player-facing docs (2.2).

## Part 2: Docs (moved)
Every README, DESCRIPTION and CHANGELOG rule and template: `DOCS_RULES.md` (sections 2.1 to 2.5). Read it before writing any doc.
````
